\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\left. \left\langle \max \{h_{\zeta _{\tilde{r}_i}}\},\min \{h_{\eta _{{\tilde{r}}_i}}\}\right\rangle \right)$$\end{document} be two 2DLPFVs, then23\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} {\tilde{r}}^-\le 2DLPFOWA({\tilde{r}}_1,{\tilde{r}}_2,\dots ,{\tilde{r}}_n)\le {\tilde{r}}^+ \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}(P4) (Commutativity) Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\tilde{r}}_i'=\left( \left\langle \dot{s}_{\theta _{{\tilde{r}}_i'}},\dot{s}_{\sigma _{\tilde{r}_i'}}\right\rangle ,\left\langle \ddot{h}_{\zeta _{{\tilde{r}}_i'}},\ddot{h}_{\eta _{{\tilde{r}}_i'}}\right\rangle \right) \ (i=1,2,\dots ,n)$$\end{document} be any permutation of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\tilde{r}}_i=\left( \left\langle \dot{s}_{\theta _{\tilde{r}_i}},\dot{s}_{\sigma _{{\tilde{r}}_i}}\right\rangle ,\left\langle \ddot{h}_{\zeta _{{\tilde{r}}_i}},\ddot{h}_{\eta _{\tilde{r}_i}}\right\rangle \right)$$\end{document}, then for any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega$$\end{document},24\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}

Remark 4.1

In the following, let us consider the 2DLPFOWA operator for some special cases of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega$$\end{document}. if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega =(1,0,\dots ,0)^T$$\end{document}, then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} 2DLPFOWA({\tilde{r}}_1,{\tilde{r}}_2,\dots ,{\tilde{r}}_n)=\tilde{r}_{(1)}=\max ({\tilde{r}}_1,{\tilde{r}}_2,\dots ,{\tilde{r}}_n) \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega =(0,0,\dots ,1)^T$$\end{document}, then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} 2DLPFOWA({\tilde{r}}_1,{\tilde{r}}_2,\dots ,{\tilde{r}}_n)=\tilde{r}_{(n)}=\min ({\tilde{r}}_1,{\tilde{r}}_2,\dots ,{\tilde{r}}_n) \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega _i=1$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega _j=1$$\end{document} for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$j\ne i$$\end{document}, then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} 2DLPFOWA({\tilde{r}}_1,{\tilde{r}}_2,\dots ,{\tilde{r}}_n)={\tilde{r}}_{(i)} \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\tilde{r}}_{(i)}$$\end{document} is the ith largest value of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{r}_i(i=1,2,\dots ,n)$$\end{document}

if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega =(1,0,\dots ,0)^T$$\end{document}, then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} 2DLPFOWA({\tilde{r}}_1,{\tilde{r}}_2,\dots ,{\tilde{r}}_n)=\tilde{r}_{(1)}=\max ({\tilde{r}}_1,{\tilde{r}}_2,\dots ,{\tilde{r}}_n) \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}

if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega =(1,0,\dots ,0)^T$$\end{document}, then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} 2DLPFOWA({\tilde{r}}_1,{\tilde{r}}_2,\dots ,{\tilde{r}}_n)=\tilde{r}_{(1)}=\max ({\tilde{r}}_1,{\tilde{r}}_2,\dots ,{\tilde{r}}_n) \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}

if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega =(0,0,\dots ,1)^T$$\end{document}, then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} 2DLPFOWA({\tilde{r}}_1,{\tilde{r}}_2,\dots ,{\tilde{r}}_n)=\tilde{r}_{(n)}=\min ({\tilde{r}}_1,{\tilde{r}}_2,\dots ,{\tilde{r}}_n) \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}

if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega =(0,0,\dots ,1)^T$$\end{document}, then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} 2DLPFOWA({\tilde{r}}_1,{\tilde{r}}_2,\dots ,{\tilde{r}}_n)=\tilde{r}_{(n)}=\min ({\tilde{r}}_1,{\tilde{r}}_2,\dots ,{\tilde{r}}_n) \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}

if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega _i=1$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega _j=1$$\end{document} for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$j\ne i$$\end{document}, then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} 2DLPFOWA({\tilde{r}}_1,{\tilde{r}}_2,\dots ,{\tilde{r}}_n)={\tilde{r}}_{(i)} \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}

if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega _i=1$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega _j=1$$\end{document} for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$j\ne i$$\end{document}, then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} 2DLPFOWA({\tilde{r}}_1,{\tilde{r}}_2,\dots ,{\tilde{r}}_n)={\tilde{r}}_{(i)} \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}

Geometric aggregation operators

In this section, we will propose some 2-dimension linguistic Pythagorean fuzzy geometric aggregation operators for 2DLPFVs, which include the 2-dimension linguistic Pythagorean fuzzy weighted geometric (2DLPFWG) operator and the 2-dimension linguistic Pythagorean fuzzy ordered weighted geometric (2DLPFOWG) operator.

Definition 4.3

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$r_i=\left( \left\langle \dot{s}_{\theta _{{\tilde{r}}_i}},\dot{s}_{\sigma _{{\tilde{r}}_i}}\right\rangle ,\left\langle \ddot{h}_{\zeta _{{\tilde{r}}_i}},\ddot{h}_{\eta _{\tilde{r}_i}}\right\rangle \right) \ (i=1,2,\dots ,n)$$\end{document} be a collection of 2DLPFVs. Then the 2-dimension linguistic Pythagorean fuzzy weighted geometric (2DLPFWG) operator is a mapping \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$2DLPFWG:\Omega ^n\rightarrow \Omega$$\end{document}, such that25\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} 2DLPFWA({\tilde{r}}_1,{\tilde{r}}_2,\dots ,{\tilde{r}}_n)=\omega _1\tilde{r}_1\otimes \omega _2{\tilde{r}}_2\otimes \dots \otimes \omega _n{\tilde{r}}_n \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega =(\omega _1,\omega _2,\dots ,\omega _n)^T$$\end{document} is the weight vector of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\tilde{r}}_i\ (i=1,2,\dots ,n)$$\end{document} with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0\le \omega _i\le 1$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sum _{i=1}^n{\omega _i}=1$$\end{document}. Especially, if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega =(1/n,1/n,\dots ,1/n)^T$$\end{document}, the 2DLPFWG operator would reduce to 2DLPFG operator as:26\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} 2DLPFG({\tilde{r}}_1,{\tilde{r}}_2,\dots ,\tilde{r}_n)=\frac{1}{n}\left( \otimes _{i=1}^n{{\tilde{r}}_i}\right) \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}

Theorem 4.5

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$r_i=\left( \left\langle \dot{s}_{\theta _{{\tilde{r}}_i}},\dot{s}_{\sigma _{{\tilde{r}}_i}}\right\rangle ,\left\langle \ddot{h}_{\zeta _{{\tilde{r}}_i}},\ddot{h}_{\eta _{\tilde{r}_i}}\right\rangle \right) \ (i=1,2,\dots ,n)$$\end{document} be a collection of n 2DLPFVs, then the aggregated value obtained by the 2DLPFWG operator is still a 2DLPFV and is given by27\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} 2DLPFWG({\tilde{r}}_1,{\tilde{r}}_2,\dots ,{\tilde{r}}_n)=&\left( \left\langle \dot{s}_{t\prod _{i=1}^n{\left( \frac{\theta _{\tilde{r}_i}}{t}\right) ^{\omega _i}}}, \dot{s}_{t\sqrt{1-\prod _{i=1}^n{\left( 1-\frac{\sigma _{\tilde{r}_i}^2}{t^2}\right) ^{\omega _i}}}}\right\rangle ,\right. \\&\left. \left\langle \ddot{h}_{\tau \prod _{i=1}^n{\left( \frac{\zeta _{\tilde{r}_i}}{\tau }\right) ^{\omega _i}}},\ddot{h}_{\tau \sqrt{1-\prod _{i=1}^n{\left( 1-\frac{\eta _{\tilde{r}_i}^2}{\tau ^2}\right) ^{\omega _i}}}}\right\rangle \right) \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega =(\omega _1,\omega _2,\dots ,\omega _n)^T$$\end{document} is the weight vector of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\tilde{r}}_i\ (i=1,2,\dots ,n)$$\end{document} with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0\le \omega _i\le 1$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sum _{i=1}^n{\omega _i}=1$$\end{document}.

Proof

The proof of this theorem is similar to that of Theorem 4.1, and hence is omitted here.

Similar to the 2DLPFWA operator, the 2DLPFWG operator has the following properties: \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square$$\end{document}

Theorem 4.6

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$r_i=\left( \left\langle \dot{s}_{\theta _{{\tilde{r}}_i}},\dot{s}_{\sigma _{{\tilde{r}}_i}}\right\rangle ,\left\langle \ddot{h}_{\zeta _{{\tilde{r}}_i}},\ddot{h}_{\eta _{\tilde{r}_i}}\right\rangle \right) \ (i=1,2,\dots ,n)$$\end{document} be a collection of 2DLPFVs, and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega =(\omega _1,\omega _2,\dots ,\omega _n)^T$$\end{document} is the weight vector of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\tilde{r}}_i\ (i=1,2,\dots ,n)$$\end{document} with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0\le \omega _i\le 1$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sum _{i=1}^n{\omega _i}=1$$\end{document}, then the 2DLPFWG operator has the following:

(P1) (Idempotency) If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\tilde{r}}_i=\left( \left\langle \dot{s}_{\theta },\dot{s}_{\sigma }\right\rangle ,\left\langle \ddot{h}_{\zeta },\ddot{h}_{\eta }\right\rangle \right)$$\end{document} for all i, then28\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} 2DLPFWG({\tilde{r}}_1,{\tilde{r}}_2,\dots ,{\tilde{r}}_n)=\left( \left\langle \dot{s}_{\theta },\dot{s}_{\sigma }\right\rangle ,\left\langle \ddot{h}_{\zeta },\ddot{h}_{\eta }\right\rangle \right) \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}(P2) (Monotonicity) Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\tilde{l}}_i=\left( \left\langle \dot{s}_{\theta _{{\tilde{l}}_i}},\dot{s}_{\sigma _{\tilde{l}_i}}\right\rangle ,\left\langle \ddot{h}_{\zeta _{{\tilde{l}}_i}},\ddot{h}_{\eta _{{\tilde{l}}_i}}\right\rangle \right) \ (i=1,2,\dots ,n)$$\end{document} be another collection of 2DLPFVs such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\tilde{r}}_i\le {\tilde{l}}_i$$\end{document} for all i, then29\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} 2DLPFWG({\tilde{r}}_1,{\tilde{r}}_2,\dots ,{\tilde{r}}_n)\le 2DLPFV(\tilde{l}_1,{\tilde{l}}_2,\dots ,{\tilde{l}}_n) \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}(P3) (Boundedness) Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\tilde{r}}^-=\left( \left\langle \min \{s_{\theta _{{\tilde{r}}_i}}\},\max \{s_{\sigma _{\tilde{r}_i}}\}\right\rangle ,\left\langle \min \{h_{\zeta _{\tilde{r}_i}}\},\max \{h_{\eta _{{\tilde{r}}_i}}\}\right\rangle \right)$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\tilde{r}}^+=\left( \left\langle \max \{s_{\theta _{\tilde{r}_i}}\},\min \{s_{\sigma _{{\tilde{r}}_i}}\}\right\rangle ,\right.$$\end{document}

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\left. \left\langle \max \{h_{\zeta _{\tilde{r}_i}}\},\min \{h_{\eta _{{\tilde{r}}_i}}\}\right\rangle \right)$$\end{document} be two 2DLPFVs, then30\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} {\tilde{r}}^-\le 2DLPFWG({\tilde{r}}_1,{\tilde{r}}_2,\dots ,{\tilde{r}}_n)\le {\tilde{r}}^+ \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}(P4) Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\tilde{l}}=\left( \left\langle \dot{s}_{\theta _{\tilde{l}}},\dot{s}_{\sigma _{{\tilde{l}}}}\right\rangle ,\left\langle \ddot{h}_{\zeta _{{\tilde{l}}}},\ddot{h}_{\eta _{{\tilde{l}}}}\right\rangle \right)$$\end{document} be another 2DLPFV, then31\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} 2DLPFWG({\tilde{r}}_1\otimes {\tilde{l}},{\tilde{r}}_2\otimes \tilde{l},\dots ,{\tilde{r}}_n\otimes {\tilde{l}}) =2DLPFWG({\tilde{r}}_1,\tilde{r}_2,\dots ,{\tilde{r}}_n)\otimes {\tilde{l}} \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}(P5) Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda >0$$\end{document} be a real number, then32\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} 2DLPFWG({\tilde{r}}_1^{\lambda },{\tilde{r}}_2^{\lambda },\dots ,\tilde{r}_n^{\lambda })=\left( 2DLPFV({\tilde{r}}_1,{\tilde{r}}_2,\dots ,\tilde{r}_n)\right) ^{\lambda } \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}(P6) Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\tilde{l}}=\left( \left\langle \dot{s}_{\theta _{\tilde{l}}},\dot{s}_{\sigma _{{\tilde{l}}}}\right\rangle ,\left\langle \ddot{h}_{\zeta _{{\tilde{l}}}},\ddot{h}_{\eta _{{\tilde{l}}}}\right\rangle \right)$$\end{document} be another 2DLPFV and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda >0$$\end{document} be a real number, then33\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned}&2DLPFWG({\tilde{r}}_1^{\lambda }\otimes {\tilde{l}},{\tilde{r}}_2^{\lambda }\otimes {\tilde{l}},\dots ,{\tilde{r}}_n^{\lambda }\otimes {\tilde{l}})\\ =&\left( 2DLPFWG({\tilde{r}}_1,{\tilde{r}}_2,\dots ,\tilde{r}_n)\right) ^{\lambda }\otimes {\tilde{l}} \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}(P7) Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\tilde{l}}_i=\left( \left\langle \dot{s}_{\theta _{\tilde{l}_i}},\dot{s}_{\sigma _{{\tilde{l}}_i}}\right\rangle ,\left\langle \ddot{h}_{\zeta _{{\tilde{l}}_i}},\ddot{h}_{\eta _{\tilde{l}_i}}\right\rangle \right) \ (i=1,2,\dots ,n)$$\end{document} be another collection of 2DLPFVs, then34\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned}&2DLPFWG({\tilde{r}}_1\otimes {\tilde{l}}_1,{\tilde{r}}_2\otimes {\tilde{l}}_2,\dots ,{\tilde{r}}_n\otimes {\tilde{l}}_n)\\ =&2DLPFWG({\tilde{r}}_1,{\tilde{r}}_2,\dots ,{\tilde{r}}_n)\otimes 2DLPFWG({\tilde{l}}_1,{\tilde{l}}_2,\dots ,{\tilde{l}}_n) \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}

Proof

The proof of Theorem 4.6 is presented in E. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square$$\end{document}

Definition 4.4

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$r_i=\left( \left\langle \dot{s}_{\theta _{{\tilde{r}}_i}},\dot{s}_{\sigma _{{\tilde{r}}_i}}\right\rangle ,\left\langle \ddot{h}_{\zeta _{{\tilde{r}}_i}},\ddot{h}_{\eta _{\tilde{r}_i}}\right\rangle \right) \ (i=1,2,\dots ,n)$$\end{document} be a collection of 2DLPFVs. Then the 2-dimension linguistic Pythagorean fuzzy ordered weighted geometric (2DLPFOWG) operator is a mapping \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$2DLPFOWG:\Omega ^n\rightarrow \Omega$$\end{document}, such that35\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} 2DLPFWG({\tilde{r}}_1,{\tilde{r}}_2,\dots ,{\tilde{r}}_n)=\omega _1\tilde{r}_{(1)}\otimes \omega _2\tilde{r}_{(2)}\otimes \dots \otimes \omega _n{\tilde{r}}_{(n)} \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\tilde{r}}_{(i)}$$\end{document} is the ith largest value of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{r}_1,{\tilde{r}}_2,\dots ,{\tilde{r}}_n$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega =(\omega _1,\omega _2,\dots ,\omega _n)^T$$\end{document} is the associated weight vector of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\tilde{r}}_{(i)}\ (i=1,2,\dots ,n)$$\end{document} with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0\le \omega _i\le 1$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sum _{i=1}^n{\omega _i}=1$$\end{document}. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square$$\end{document}

Theorem 4.7

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$r_i=\left( \left\langle \dot{s}_{\theta _{{\tilde{r}}_i}},\dot{s}_{\sigma _{{\tilde{r}}_i}}\right\rangle ,\left\langle \ddot{h}_{\zeta _{{\tilde{r}}_i}},\ddot{h}_{\eta _{\tilde{r}_i}}\right\rangle \right) \ (i=1,2,\dots ,n)$$\end{document} be a collection of n 2DLPFVs, then the aggregated value obtained by the 2DLPFOWG operator is still a 2DLPFV and is given by36\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} 2DLPFOWG({\tilde{r}}_1,{\tilde{r}}_2,\dots ,{\tilde{r}}_n)=&\left( \left\langle \dot{s}_{t\prod _{i=1}^n{\left( \frac{\theta _{{\tilde{r}}_{(i)}}}{t}\right) ^{\omega _i}}},\dot{s}_{t\sqrt{1-\prod _{i=1}^n{\left( 1-\frac{\sigma _{{\tilde{r}}_{(i)}}^2}{t^2}\right) ^{\omega _i}}}}\right\rangle ,\right. \\&\left. \left\langle \ddot{h}_{\tau \prod _{i=1}^n{\left( \frac{\zeta _{\tilde{r}_{(i)}}}{\tau }\right) ^{\omega _i}}},\ddot{h}_{\tau \sqrt{1-\prod _{i=1}^n{\left( 1-\frac{\eta _{\tilde{r}_{(i)}}^2}{\tau ^2}\right) ^{\omega _i}}}}\right\rangle \right) \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega =(\omega _1,\omega _2,\dots ,\omega _n)^T$$\end{document} is the associated weight vector of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\tilde{r}}_{(i)}\ (i=1,2,\dots ,n)$$\end{document} with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0\le \omega _i\le 1$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sum _{i=1}^n{\omega _i}=1$$\end{document}.

Proof

The proof is similar to that of Theorem 4.3, and hence is omitted here.

The 2DLPFOWG operator also satisfies the properties given in Theorem 4.3. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square$$\end{document}

Remark 4.2

In the following, let us consider the 2DLPFOWG operator for some special cases of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega$$\end{document}. if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega =(1,0,\dots ,0)^T$$\end{document}, then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} ({\tilde{r}}_1,{\tilde{r}}_2,\dots ,{\tilde{r}}_n) \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega =(0,0,\dots ,1)^T$$\end{document}, then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} 2DLPFOWG({\tilde{r}}_1,{\tilde{r}}_2,\dots ,{\tilde{r}}_n)=\tilde{r}_{(n)}=\min ({\tilde{r}}_1,{\tilde{r}}_2,\dots ,{\tilde{r}}_n) \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega _i=1$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega _j=1$$\end{document} for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$j\ne i$$\end{document}, then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} 2DLPFOWG({\tilde{r}}_1,{\tilde{r}}_2,\dots ,{\tilde{r}}_n)={\tilde{r}}_{(i)} \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{r}_i(i=1,2,\dots ,n)$$\end{document}

if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega =(1,0,\dots ,0)^T$$\end{document}, then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} 2DLPFOWG({\tilde{r}}_1,{\tilde{r}}_2,\dots ,{\tilde{r}}_n)=\tilde{r}_{(1)}=\max ({\tilde{r}}_1,{\tilde{r}}_2,\dots ,{\tilde{r}}_n) \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}

if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega =(1,0,\dots ,0)^T$$\end{document}, then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} 2DLPFOWG({\tilde{r}}_1,{\tilde{r}}_2,\dots ,{\tilde{r}}_n)=\tilde{r}_{(1)}=\max ({\tilde{r}}_1,{\tilde{r}}_2,\dots ,{\tilde{r}}_n) \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}

if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega =(0,0,\dots ,1)^T$$\end{document}, then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} 2DLPFOWG({\tilde{r}}_1,{\tilde{r}}_2,\dots ,{\tilde{r}}_n)=\tilde{r}_{(n)}=\min ({\tilde{r}}_1,{\tilde{r}}_2,\dots ,{\tilde{r}}_n) \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}

if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega =(0,0,\dots ,1)^T$$\end{document}, then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} 2DLPFOWG({\tilde{r}}_1,{\tilde{r}}_2,\dots ,{\tilde{r}}_n)=\tilde{r}_{(n)}=\min ({\tilde{r}}_1,{\tilde{r}}_2,\dots ,{\tilde{r}}_n) \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}

if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega _i=1$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega _j=1$$\end{document} for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$j\ne i$$\end{document}, then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} 2DLPFOWG({\tilde{r}}_1,{\tilde{r}}_2,\dots ,{\tilde{r}}_n)={\tilde{r}}_{(i)} \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}

if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega _i=1$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega _j=1$$\end{document} for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$j\ne i$$\end{document}, then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} 2DLPFOWG({\tilde{r}}_1,{\tilde{r}}_2,\dots ,{\tilde{r}}_n)={\tilde{r}}_{(i)} \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}

Lemma 4.1

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$x_i\ge 0,a_i>0(i=1,2,\dots ,n)$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sum _{i=1}^n{a_i}=1$$\end{document}, then37\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} \prod _{i=1}^n{\left( x_i\right) ^{a_i}}\le \sum _{i=1}^n{a_ix_i} \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}where equality holds only if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$x_1=x_2=\dots =x_n$$\end{document}.

Based on Lemma 4.1, we can derive the following theorem.

Theorem 4.8

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\tilde{r}}_i=\left( \left\langle \dot{s}_{\theta _{{\tilde{r}}_i}},\dot{s}_{\sigma _{{\tilde{r}}_i}}\right\rangle ,\left\langle \ddot{h}_{\zeta _{{\tilde{r}}_i}},\ddot{h}_{\eta _{\tilde{r}_i}}\right\rangle \right) \ (i=1,2,\dots ,n)$$\end{document} be a collection of 2DLPFVs, there is

(i) \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$2DLPFWA({\tilde{r}}_1,{\tilde{r}}_2,\dots ,{\tilde{r}}_n)\ge 2DLPFWG({\tilde{r}}_1,{\tilde{r}}_2,\dots ,{\tilde{r}}_n)$$\end{document}.

(ii) \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$2DLPFOWA({\tilde{r}}_1,{\tilde{r}}_2,\dots ,{\tilde{r}}_n)\ge 2DLPFOWG({\tilde{r}}_1,{\tilde{r}}_2,\dots ,{\tilde{r}}_n)$$\end{document}.

where equality holds if and only if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\tilde{r}}_1=\tilde{r}_2=\dots ={\tilde{r}}_n$$\end{document}.

Proof

Proof of Theorem 4.8 is presented in F. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square$$\end{document}

Remark 4.3

The four aggregation operators introduced in this section are selected from the class of fundamental operators. Their simplicity serves an important purpose: the central contribution of this study lies in the introduction of the 2DLPFV and the establishment of its algebraic and representational properties. Using well-understood operators provides a transparent and verifiable baseline for illustrating the mechanics of the proposed framework. While more sophisticated operators, such as Bonferroni mean, Heronian mean, or Muirhead mean, can capture inter-attribute dependencies and attitudinal effects, they are intentionally left for future work. Including such complexity at this stage would risk obscuring the novelty of the 2DLPFV itself. This study therefore prioritizes clarity and reproducibility, while laying a foundation for subsequent extensions that incorporate advanced operators.

A novel multi-criteria decision-making method for UAV contribution assessment

Problem description

With the rapid development of UAV swarm technology, groups of UAVs are increasingly deployed to perform complex operations. Within a swarm, individual UAVs differ in their influence on the collective mission outcome. The degree to which a UAV affects the operational effectiveness of the swarm is referred to as its contribution degree. Formally, the contribution degree of the ith UAV can be expressed as38\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \alpha _i=\frac{\xi _i-\xi _0}{\xi _0}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\xi _0$$\end{document} is the operational effectiveness of the original UAV swarm, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\xi _i$$\end{document} is the operational effectiveness of the swarm including the ith UAV, and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha _i$$\end{document} denotes the contribution degree of the ith UAV.

Since the assessment of contribution is inherently based on operational effectiveness, the UAV contribution assessment problem can be naturally framed as a MCDM problem. Consider m UAV alternatives \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$U=\{U_1,U_2,\dots ,U_m\}$$\end{document}, corresponding to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$m+1$$\end{document} swarm configurations \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$A=\{A_0,A_1,A_2,\dots ,A_m\}$$\end{document}, where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$A_0$$\end{document} denotes the original swarm and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$A_i$$\end{document} represents the renewed swarm including the ith UAV. Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C=\{C_1,C_2,\dots ,C_n\}$$\end{document} be the set of n evaluation criteria, with weight vector \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega =(\omega _1,\omega _2,\dots ,\omega _n)^T$$\end{document}, satisfying \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0\le \omega _j\le 1$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sum _{j=1}^n \omega _j=1$$\end{document}. Suppose that there are l experts \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$E=\{E_1,E_2,\dots ,E_l\}$$\end{document}, with weight vector \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$w=(w_1,w_2,\dots ,w_l)^T$$\end{document}, satisfying \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0\le w_k\le 1$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sum _{k=1}^l w_k=1$$\end{document}. The assessment provided by expert \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$E_k$$\end{document} on alternative \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$U_i$$\end{document} with respect to criterion \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C_j$$\end{document} is expressed using 2DLPFV as:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} {\tilde{r}}_{i,j}^k=\left( \langle \dot{s}_{\theta _{i,j}}^k,\dot{s}_{\sigma _{i,j}}^k\rangle ,\langle \ddot{h}_{\zeta _{i,j}}^k,\ddot{h}_{\eta _{i,j}}^k\rangle \right) , \end{aligned}$$\end{document}where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\dot{s}_{\theta _{i,j}}^k,\dot{s}_{\sigma _{i,j}}^k\in {\hat{S}}=\{s_p|p\in [0,t]\}$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\ddot{h}_{\zeta _{i,j}}^k,\ddot{h}_{\eta _{i,j}}^k\in {\hat{H}}=\{h_q|q\in [0,\tau ]\}$$\end{document}. The assessments of each expert can be summarized as a two-dimension linguistic Pythagorean fuzzy decision matrix \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$R_k=[\tilde{r}_{i,j}^k]_{(m+1)\times n}$$\end{document}. On this basis, a novel MCDM method for UAV contribution assessment is constructed using the proposed 2DLPFV aggregation operators.

To illustrate the necessity of this representation, consider the evaluation of two UAVs on the criterion of communications robustness. UAV-A is judged by experts as “high” in robustness, but with low confidence because it has only been tested in a few jamming scenarios. UAV-B is judged as “medium” in robustness, but with high confidence owing to extensive operational testing. In a conventional LPFS, both evaluations collapse onto a single axis, making the difference in confidence invisible unless external weighting is introduced. 2DLPFSs allow a second axis for confidence, but their linear constraint often underrepresents hesitancy when expert opinions diverge. By contrast, the proposed 2DLPFS captures both the evaluative judgment and the associated reliability within a unified Pythagorean framework. This enables decision makers to rationally prefer UAV-B over UAV-A when reliability is critical, reflecting the way experts naturally distinguish between what they believe and how strongly they believe it. Figure 1 shows a practical case of 2DLPFS compared with LPFS.Figure 1One-dimensional versus two-dimensional representation of expert assessments. Left (1D, LPFS): two experts assign the same linguistic evaluation (“Moderate”), which collapses to the same point on the evaluation axis and discards differences in confidence. Right (2D, 2DLPFV): the same evaluation is represented on the horizontal axis (type I), while confidence (type II) appears on the vertical axis, separating a high-confidence judgement (Expert A) from a lower-confidence one (Expert B). This prevents information loss and enables reliability-aware aggregation.

One-dimensional versus two-dimensional representation of expert assessments. Left (1D, LPFS): two experts assign the same linguistic evaluation (“Moderate”), which collapses to the same point on the evaluation axis and discards differences in confidence. Right (2D, 2DLPFV): the same evaluation is represented on the horizontal axis (type I), while confidence (type II) appears on the vertical axis, separating a high-confidence judgement (Expert A) from a lower-confidence one (Expert B). This prevents information loss and enables reliability-aware aggregation.

MCDM approach

In this section, a novel UAV contribution assessment method is proposed based on 2DLPFV to better handle the subjective and uncertain cognitive information of the experts. Figure 2 shows the flowchart of the proposed method, and the detailed steps of the proposed method are as follows:Figure 2Process of the proposed UAV contribution assessment method.

Process of the proposed UAV contribution assessment method.

Step 1: Obtain the assessment information

In UAV contribution assessment, the assessment information describes the experts’ judgments on the performance of different UAV swarms with regard to different criteria, which is expressed by 2DLPFVs. In this step, the 2-dimension linguistic Pythagorean fuzzy decision matrix \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$R_k=[{\tilde{r}}_{i,j}^k]_{(m+1)\times n}$$\end{document} is constructed based on the assessments of the expert on different alternatives with regard to different criteria as:39\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} R_k= \begin{bmatrix} {\tilde{r}}_{0,1}^k & {\tilde{r}}_{0,2}^k & \cdots & {\tilde{r}}_{0,j}^k & \cdots & {\tilde{r}}_{0,n}^k \\ {\tilde{r}}_{1,1}^k & {\tilde{r}}_{1,2}^k & \cdots & {\tilde{r}}_{1,j}^k & \cdots & {\tilde{r}}_{1,n}^k \\ {\tilde{r}}_{2,1}^k & {\tilde{r}}_{2,2}^k & \cdots & {\tilde{r}}_{2,j}^k & \cdots & {\tilde{r}}_{2,n}^k \\ \vdots & \vdots & \ddots & \vdots & \ddots & \vdots \\ {\tilde{r}}_{i,1}^k & {\tilde{r}}_{i,2}^k & \cdots & {\tilde{r}}_{i,j}^k & \cdots & {\tilde{r}}_{i,n}^k \\ \vdots & \vdots & \ddots & \vdots & \ddots & \vdots \\ {\tilde{r}}_{m,1}^k & {\tilde{r}}_{m,2}^k & \cdots & {\tilde{r}}_{m,j}^k & \cdots & {\tilde{r}}_{m,n}^k \\ \end{bmatrix} \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Step 2: Construct the aggregated 2-dimension linguistic Pythagorean fuzzy decision matrix

Step 6: Determine the rank of the UAV alternatives

According to the contribution degree, all UAV alternatives \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$U_i$$\end{document} could be ranked in descending order, corresponding to their superiority.

Case study

To illustrate the process of the proposed method, a practical case of UAV contribution degree assessment and selection is presented.

Problem description

UAV swarms are increasingly deployed in complex operations such as surveillance, reconnaissance, and communication support. Effective deployment requires not only evaluating the swarm as a whole but also assessing the contribution of each individual UAV to the collective mission. This problem is inherently multi-criteria, as contribution depends on diverse factors such as endurance, sensing capability, communication robustness, and survivability under adversarial conditions. Furthermore, evaluations are usually based on subjective expert judgment, which is inevitably uncertain.

A key challenge in this context is that expert judgments consist of two distinct aspects: (i) the evaluation itself, e.g., that a UAV’s defense capability is “moderately good,” and (ii) the confidence or reliability with which the expert provides this evaluation. Conventional one-dimensional models, such as the LPFS, can represent evaluative uncertainty but cannot distinguish between confident and hesitant judgments. Consequently, two assessments with the same linguistic value but different confidence levels are treated as identical, leading to loss of critical information.

Consider the following example. An experienced engineer and a junior analyst both evaluate the “defense capability” of a UAV as “moderately good.” The engineer expresses this evaluation with very high confidence, while the analyst does so with only fair confidence. In a one-dimensional framework, these two assessments are indistinguishable, meaning that hesitant judgments may be given the same weight as confident ones. Such oversimplification can bias aggregation and distort the final ranking of UAVs, which is particularly problematic in high-stakes applications where reliability of information is as important as the evaluation itself.

To overcome this limitation, the proposed 2DLPFV introduces an explicit second dimension to represent confidence (type II information), alongside the first dimension that captures the evaluative judgment (type I information). By keeping these two types of information distinct yet integrated within the same admissible domain, the 2DLPFV enables reliability-aware aggregation of expert opinions. This dual-axis design ensures that uncertain or hesitant judgments are moderated, while highly confident judgments are appropriately reinforced. Hence, the two-dimensional representation is not an incremental extension but a necessary enhancement to accurately and rationally solve the UAV contribution assessment problem under uncertainty.

Results

With the rapid development of control and automation technologies, the application of UAV swarms in surveillance operations is increasing, and many different UAVs have been developed. Suppose there is an existing (original) UAV swarm \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$A_0$$\end{document} for the surveillance operations, due to the limitation of composed UAVs, its operational effectiveness has been significantly limited, and a new UAV is required to be added to this UAV swarm. As the required UAV shall work within the UAV swarm, the contribution degree is used to assess and select the best alternative. According to the aforementioned definition of UAV contribution degree, the contribution degree of the UAV indicates the effect it has on the operational effectiveness of the UAV swarm, that is to say, the contribution assessment is based on the assessment of the operational effectiveness of the UAV swarm. Thus, proper criteria for evaluating the UAV swarm should be determined.

Based on the analysis of the UAV and the UAV swarm, four criteria are considered in this study, namely, Information processing capability (\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C_1$$\end{document}), Defense capability (\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C_2$$\end{document}), Maneuver capability (\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C_3$$\end{document}) and Operation efficiency (\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C_4$$\end{document}), and the weight vector of the criteria is obtained as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega =(0.4,0.2,0.3,0.1)^T$$\end{document} based on the knowledge of experts. Step 1: After assessing the evaluation criteria, the 2-dimension linguistic Pythagorean fuzzy decision matrix \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$R_k=[{\tilde{r}}_{i,j}^k]_{(m+1)\times Step 2: By utilizing the normal distribution method, the weight vector associated with the experts can be obtained as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$w=(w_1,w_2,w_3)^T=(0.243,0.514,0.243)^T$$\end{document}. Then, by using the 2DLPFOWA operator, the assessments of different experts could be aggregated to obtain the aggregated decision matrix \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$R=[\tilde{r}_{i,j}]_{5\times 4}$$\end{document}, and the results are shown in Table 5.Table 5The aggregated 2-dimension linguistic Pythagorean fuzzy decision matrix using the Step 3: Aggregate the assessments \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\tilde{r}}_{i,j}$$\end{document} of different criteria for each UAV swarm \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$A_i$$\end{document} using the 2DLPFWA operator in Eq. (11), and the aggregated 2-dimension linguistic Pythagorean fuzzy assessments \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\tilde{r}}_i$$\end{document} of different UAV swarms could be obtained as:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{gathered} {\tilde{r}}_0=(\langle \dot{s}_{2.5814},\dot{s}_{6.3979}\rangle ,\langle \ddot{h}_{3.3580},\ddot{h}_{3.4918}\rangle ) \\ {\tilde{r}}_1=(\langle \dot{s}_{4.9940},\dot{s}_{4.0902}\rangle ,\langle \ddot{h}_{4.4224},\ddot{h}_{2.6960}\rangle ) \\ {\tilde{r}}_2=(\langle \dot{s}_{6.6698},\dot{s}_{2.5903}\rangle ,\langle \ddot{h}_{4.9337},\ddot{h}_{1.5413}\rangle ) \\ {\tilde{r}}_3=(\langle \dot{s}_{5.5571},\dot{s}_{3.4189}\rangle ,\langle \ddot{h}_{3.9073},\ddot{h}_{2.7494}\rangle ) \\ {\tilde{r}}_4=(\langle \dot{s}_{5.7046},\dot{s}_{3.0359}\rangle ,\langle \ddot{h}_{4.0411},\ddot{h}_{3.3455}\rangle ) \end{gathered} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Step 4: Calculate the score value \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\rho _i$$\end{document} of each UAV swarm based on the aggregated assessment of the UAV swarm as:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{gathered} \rho _0=u_{0.3364},\ \rho _1=u_{0.6151},\ \rho _2=u_{0.8001},\ \rho _3=u_{0.6281},\ \rho _4=u_{0.6243} \end{gathered} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Hence, the operational effectiveness \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\xi _i$$\end{document} of each UAV swarm can be obtained as:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{gathered} \xi _0=0.3364,\ \xi _1={0.6151}, \xi _2={0.8001}, \xi _3={0.6281}, \xi _4={0.6243} \end{gathered} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Based on the operational effectiveness, the performance of each UAV swarm could be obtained. As the performance of different UAV swarms is affected by the inclusion of different UAVs, the UAV contribution degree could be calculated based on operational effectiveness.

Step 5: Calculate the contribution degree \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha _i$$\end{document} of the UAV \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\{U_1,U_2,U_3,U_4\}$$\end{document} according to the operational effectiveness of the UAV swarm as:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{gathered} \alpha _1=0.8284,\ \alpha _2=1.3783, \alpha _3=0.8671, \alpha _4=0.8559 \end{gathered} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Step 6: Rank all the UAV alternatives according to the contribution degree as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$U_2\succ U_3\succ U_4\succ U_1$$\end{document}.

The contribution degree indicates the performance of the UAV within the UAV swarm, hence, the UAV with the highest contribution degree, i.e., \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$U_2$$\end{document} is selected as the replacement option for the UAV swarm.

It should be noted that the contribution degree denotes the effects the UAV has in the UAV swarm, and a larger contribution degree value clearly indicates better performance. Thus, as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$U_2$$\end{document} is assessed to have a contribution degree of 1.3783, it obviously outperforms other UAVs in terms of cooperating with the UAV swarm, and it is selected as the replacement option. In addition, the obtained ranking of the UAVs could provide insights into the importance and superiority of different UAVs with regard to their performance.

In the above example, the 2DLPFOWA operator and the 2DLPFWA operator are used in Step 2 and Step 3, respectively, which could be changed to different operators. In the following, we will illustrate the process of the proposed method using the 2DLPFOWG operator and the 2DLPFWG operator.

Step 1: Construct the 2-dimension linguistic Pythagorean fuzzy decision matrix \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$R_k=[{\tilde{r}}_{i,j}^k]_{(m+1)\times n}$$\end{document} of each expert, as listed in Tables 2, 3 and 4.

Step 2: By utilizing the 2DLPFOWG operator, aggregate the assessments of different experts to obtain the aggregated decision matrix \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$R=[{\tilde{r}}_{i,j}]_{5\times 4}$$\end{document}, presented in Table 6.Table 6The aggregated 2-dimension linguistic Pythagorean fuzzy decision matrix using the 2DLPFOWG Comparison between the contribution degrees of different aggregation operators.

Comparison between the normalized contribution degrees of different aggregation operators.

From Fig. 4, it can be found that the line representing 2DLPFOWG+2DLPFWG, i.e., using the 2DLPFOWG operator in Step 2 and the 2DLPFWG operator in Step 3, is deeper than others, which indicates a higher degree of difference in the normalized contribution degree. Comparison of distinguishability between neighboring UAVs using different aggregation operators.

Table 8 presents the distinguishability results between neighboring UAVs using different aggregation operators, where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\gamma _{avg}$$\end{document} is the mean value of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\gamma _1$$\end{document}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\gamma _2$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\gamma _3$$\end{document}. Larger distinguishability values often indicate better assessment of the UAV and higher assessment efficiency. As can be found in Table 8, when using the 2DLPFOWG operator in Step 2 and the 2DLPFWG operator in Step 3, a larger mean value of the distinguishability could be obtained, which means by using the 2DLPFOWG+2DLPFWG operator, we can obtain better distinguishability for assessment results. Moreover, it can be found that the 2DLPFOWA+2DLPFWA operator has the smallest distinguishability values, and the 2DLPFOWG+2DLPFWA operator has larger distinguishability values than the 2DLPFOWG+2DLPFWA operator, indicating that the aggregation operator used in Step 2 has bigger impact on the final outcome than the aggregation operator used in Step 3. In general, it can be said that the 2DLPFOWG+2DLPFWG operator is more suitable for UAV contribution assessment, though other aggregation operators also could provide accurate ranking orders and promising assessment results.

Validity analysis

In order to validate the proposed method, a validity analysis is conducted. The performance of the proposed method is assessed in the following aspects.

Aspect 1: An effective decision-making method should have the same optimal alternative if a non-optimal alternative is replaced by a worse one without other changes.

Aspect 2: An effective decision-making method should satisfy the property of transitivity, that is, if alternative A is preferred to alternative B, and alternative B is preferred to alternative C, then alternative A should be preferred to alternative C.

Aspect 3: If a complex decision-making problem is decomposed into several sub-problems, then the combined results obtained from these sub-problems using the decision-making method should be the same as the ranking result of the original problem.

In order to determine the validity of the proposed method, the following tests are conducted.
