By using the proposed method, the ranking of the UAVs is obtained as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$U_2\succ U_3\succ U_1\succ U_4'$$\end{document}, where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$U_2$$\end{document} remains the optimal alternative. Therefore, from the results, it can be found that when \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$U_4$$\end{document} is replaced by a worse alternative (\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$U_4'$$\end{document}), the optimal alternative remains the same. The same conclusion could be obtained for other non-optimal alternatives such as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$U_1$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$U_3$$\end{document}. Thus, this indicates that the proposed method is validated through Aspect 1.

Aspects 2 and 3: The original decision-making problem is decomposed into three sub-problems that contain \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\{U_1,U_2,U_3\}$$\end{document}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\{U_1,U_2,U_4\}$$\end{document}, and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\{U_2,U_3,U_4\}$$\end{document}, respectively. By using the proposed method to solve these sub-problems, the ranking orders of these problems can be obtained as:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} U_2\succ U_3\succ U_1 \\ U_2\succ U_4\succ U_1 \\ U_2\succ U_3\succ U_4 \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}By combining the results of these sub-problems, the overall result is obtained as:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} U_2\succ U_3\succ U_4\succ U_1 \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}which is in line with the result of the original problem. Therefore, the proposed method is validated through Aspects 2 and 3.

Comparative analysis

In order to show the advantages of the proposed method, two sets of comparative analyses are conducted. First, the 2DLPFV is compared with several uncertainty representation methods to show its advantages in handling uncertain information. Second, the proposed UAV contribution assessment method is compared to other methods to show the superiority of the proposed method.

Comparative analysis on the handling of uncertain information

In this study, the 2DLPFV is introduced to represent the uncertain judgments of experts. In order to show its advantages in handling uncertain information, the proposed method is compared with 2DLV and 2DLIFV. The relationship between 2DLV, 2DLIFV and 2DLPFV for I class information.

The relationship between 2DLV, 2DLIFV and 2DLPFV for II class information.

From Figs. 5 and 6, it can be found that instead of limiting each class to be singletons such as 2DLV or limiting the sum of the subscript of the membership degree and the non-membership degree to be less than t, i.e., the number of linguistic terms, such as 2DLIFV, the proposed 2DLPFV allows the square sum of the subscript of the membership degree and the non-membership degree to be less than t. Due to that modification, 2DLPFV could have a larger domain, hence, it could be more effective in dealing with uncertain information than 2DLV and 2DLIFV.

Comparative analysis on UAV contribution assessment

In order to verify the practicality and effectiveness of the proposed method, we compare the results of the proposed method with the methods introduced by Liu and Yu52, Verma and Merigo47, Zhu and Zhao48, and Li et al.53]. For comparison, we consider two aspects. First, different methods are applied to the above case for UAV contribution assessment, and the contributions of different UAVs, namely, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$A_1$$\end{document}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$A_2$$\end{document}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$A_3$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$A_4$$\end{document}, are obtained. Second, the purpose of UAV contribution assessment is to determine the most suitable alternative among all four UAV alternatives, hence, the UAVs are ranked according to their contribution degrees. UAV contribution assessment results of different methods.

Firstly, the results of the proposed method are compared with that of the method proposed by Liu and Yu52, which is based on the 2-dimension uncertain linguistic power generalized weighted aggregation (2DULPGWA) operator. Since it can only deal with 2-dimension uncertain linguistic information, we have modified the assessments of different experts to 2DULVs, where the assessment \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\left( \left\langle \dot{s}_{\theta _{i,j}}^k,\dot{s}_{\sigma _{i,j}}^k\right\rangle ,\left\langle \ddot{h}_{\zeta _{i,j}}^k,\ddot{h}_{\eta _{i,j}}^k\right\rangle \right)$$\end{document} is changed to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\left( \left\langle \dot{s}_{\theta _{i,j}}^k,\dot{s}_{\sqrt{t^2-(\sigma _{i,j})^2}}^k\right\rangle ,\left\langle \ddot{h}_{\zeta _{i,j}}^k,\ddot{h}_{\sqrt{\tau ^2-(\eta _{i,j})^2}}^k\right\rangle \right)$$\end{document}, hence, the 2DLPF decision matrices of different experts could be transformed to the corresponding 2DUL matrices. Besides, the same expert weights and criteria weights are derived from the above example. Based on the 2DULPGWA operator, the contribution degrees of the UAVs are calculated as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha _1=0.2911$$\end{document}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha _2=0.6922$$\end{document}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha _3=0.5450$$\end{document}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha _4=0.1028$$\end{document}, which can be ranked as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$U_2\succ U_3\succ U_1\succ U_4$$\end{document}. Like the proposed method, UAV \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$U_2$$\end{document} is ranked as the best option, and UAV \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$U_3$$\end{document} is the second-best one. There are some differences in the ranking of UAV \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$U_1$$\end{document} and UAV \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$U_4$$\end{document}, in the proposed method, UAV \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$U_4$$\end{document} is better than UAV \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$U_1$$\end{document}, while in Liu’s method, UAV \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$U_4$$\end{document} is inferior to UAV \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$U_1$$\end{document}, and this is due to the difference in expressing the assessment and the use of different operational rules for aggregating 2-dimension uncertain linguistic information. Compared with the method proposed by Liu and Yu52, the proposed method has the following two advantages: one is that by using the 2DLPFV, the proposed method is more flexible in reflecting the uncertain assessments, as both the membership degree and the non-membership degree are considered; another is that the proposed method treats the II class information in the same way as the I class information in the aggregation, which could provide more flexible and comprehensive results.

The results of the proposed method are then compared with the method proposed by Verma and Merigo47, where the 2-dimension linguistic intuitionistic fuzzy information is used. Since Verma’s method is based on 2DLIFV, that is, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\theta +\sigma \le 1$$\end{document}, we have reduced the 2DLPFV assessments to 2DLIFVs, where the assessment \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\left( \left\langle \dot{s}_{\theta _{i,j}}^k,\dot{s}_{\sigma _{i,j}}^k\right\rangle ,\left\langle \ddot{h}_{\zeta _{i,j}}^k,\ddot{h}_{\eta _{i,j}}^k\right\rangle \right)$$\end{document} is reduced to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\left( \left\langle \dot{s}_{\theta _{i,j}}^k,\dot{s}_{\sqrt{\sigma _{i,j}^2/t}}^k\right\rangle ,\left\langle \ddot{h}_{\zeta _{i,j}}^k,\ddot{h}_{\sqrt{\zeta _{i,j}^2/\tau }}^k\right\rangle \right)$$\end{document}, hence, the 2DLPF decision matrices of different experts could be transformed to the corresponding 2DLIF matrices. Besides, the expert weights and criteria weights remain the same. Based on the 2DLIFWA operator, the contributions degrees of the UAVs are obtained as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha _1=0.6783$$\end{document}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha _2=1.2042$$\end{document}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha _3=0.6855$$\end{document}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha _4=0.6910$$\end{document}, and the ranking can be obtained based on the contribution degrees as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$U_2\succ U_4\succ U_3\succ U_1$$\end{document}. Like the proposed method, UAV \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$U_2$$\end{document} is the best and UAV \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$U_1$$\end{document} is the worst. However, there are some differences in the ranking order of UAVs \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$U_3$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$U_4$$\end{document}, in the proposed method UAV \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$U_4$$\end{document} is assessed to be better than UAV \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$U_3$$\end{document}, whereas in Verma’s method, UAV \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$U_3$$\end{document} is inferior to UAV \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$U_4$$\end{document}, and that could be caused by the uncertain assessment in the form of 2DLIFVs. In the proposed method, the assessments of the experts are expressed using 2DLPFVs, which allows the experts to provide more intuitive and comprehensive assessments, while Verma’s method uses the 2DLIFVs, which have limited capability in representing uncertain assessments compared to 2DLPFVs. Compared with the method proposed by Verma and Merigo47, the proposed method is more flexible and reliable in representing the uncertain assessments of the experts.

Moreover, the results of the proposed method are compared with two advanced MCDM methods, namely the 2DLIF-PROMETHEE and 2DLIF-MULTIMOORA approaches. As shown in Table 10, both comparative methods yield the ranking \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$U_2 \succ U_3 \succ U_4 \succ U_1$$\end{document}, which is identical to the result produced by the proposed method. This agreement further confirms the effectiveness and reliability of the proposed framework. While the 2DLIF-PROMETHEE and 2DLIF-MULTIMOORA methods are developed on 2DLIFVs, the proposed method is built on 2DLPFVs, which provide greater flexibility in handling uncertainty and hesitancy in expert evaluations.

In addition, a consistency test is conducted using Spearman’s rank correlation coefficients. The coefficients obtained are \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(0.8,\,0.8,\,1.0,\,1.0)$$\end{document} for the comparative methods. Since these values are close to or equal to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$+1$$\end{document}, the results show strong positive correlation, indicating high consistency between the rankings derived from the proposed method and those from the benchmark methods. This consistency provides empirical evidence of the stability and logical soundness of the proposed aggregation operators.

From the comparative analysis, the following observations are obtained:

(1) In comparison with other methods, the proposed approach, leveraging 2DLPFVs to represent uncertain expert assessments, enables a more effective and flexible expression of expert judgments. This enhancement in representation contributes to the method’s robustness and adaptability.

(2) Across all three methods considered, UAV alternative \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$U_2$$\end{document} consistently emerges as the most optimal choice. This consistency in results suggests that, for UAV contribution assessment, the proposed method provides relatively reliable and stable outcomes.

The comparative results underscore the effectiveness of the proposed approach in not only capturing the nuances of expert assessments but also in consistently identifying UAV alternatives with superior contribution attributes.

Discussions and managerial implications

Discussion

Focusing on the representation of uncertain cognitive information and the assessment of UAV contribution, this study tries to provide answers to the following questions:

RQ1: How to enhance the effectiveness and flexibility of the modeling of subjective and uncertain cognitive information with linguistic representation?

RQ2: How to properly assess UAV contribution under uncertainty based on the judgments of experts?

To this end, by integrating LPFV and 2DLV, we introduced the 2DLPFV, and proposed a novel UAV contribution assessment method based on 2DLPFV. For the above problems, we obtain the following findings.

For the first question, by combining LPFV and 2DLV, the newly introduced 2DLPFV could have the advantages of both methods. Specifically, the 2DLPFV has two dimensions of information, for the I class, LPFV is adopted to represent the linguistic cognitive information of the experts’ judgments about the alternative, for the II class, LPFV is used to represent the linguistic cognitive information of the experts’ judgments on the reliability of the given result. Hence, the 2DLPFV could express both the assessment itself and the reliability of the assessment, which allows more flexible expression of subjective information. Moreover, as LPFV is used in the 2 dimensions, the 2DLPFV could allow more effective and reliable representation of uncertain information through the membership function and the non-membership function. Therefore, by introducing 2DLPFV, this study presents an effective and flexible way to model subjective and uncertain cognitive information with linguistic representation.

For the second question, since UAV contribution assessment relies heavily on experts’ judgments and experts’ judgments are often subjective and uncertain, this study proposed a novel UAV contribution assessment method based on 2DLPFV. The assessment of UAV contribution is represented using 2DLPFV, and the 2DLPFOWA and the 2DLPFOWG operators are utilized to aggregate the assessments of different experts. By using the proposed method, a practical case on UAV contribution assessment and selection is presented, where four different UAVs are assessed against four attributes. From the results, it can be found that the proposed method is capable of providing reasonable and reliable results under uncertainty. Therefore, by proposing the novel UAV contribution assessment method, this study introduces an effective approach for assessing UAV contribution under uncertainty.

Managerial implications

In this study, the 2DLPFV is introduced, and a novel decision-making approach based on 2DLPFV is proposed for UAV contribution assessment under uncertainty. The proposed method could provide further insights into the representation and handling of uncertain and linguistic information in decision-making problems. From the results of this study, some managerial implications could be obtained in this study as follows.

(1) The introduction of 2DLPFV provides a novel and effective approach for decision-making under uncertainty. This novel representation offers a flexible means to model subjective and uncertain cognitive information, enhancing the decision-making process in complex and uncertain environments.

(2) The exploration and discussion of operations related to 2DLPFV contribute to the validation and application of this model. By introducing operators tailored for 2DLPFV, this study establishes a novel framework for modeling and computing with uncertain cognitive information, providing practical guidance for decision-makers.

(3) The utilization of 2DLPFV for assessing UAV contribution based on expert judgments offers a convenient and flexible approach. This method enables the gathering and expression of more comprehensive and reliable information from experts in practical applications. This study demonstrates the potential of 2DLPFV in identifying the most suitable UAVs according to their contribution degrees.

(4) The results of this study underscore the reliability and value of the proposed method in UAV contribution assessment. This information is particularly pertinent for potential UAV users, offering a basis for making more reasonable and comprehensive decisions when selecting UAVs for replacement. This study contributes to the improvement of decision-making processes in UAV-related applications.

(5) The operators employed in this study represent fundamental forms of compensatory and conjunctive aggregation. While this choice ensures transparency and allows the effects of the proposed 2DLPFV representation to be clearly isolated, it is recognized that advanced operators can enrich the framework. In particular, operators such as Bonferroni mean or Heronian mean could capture inter-criterion correlations and provide more flexible attitudinal modeling. This confirms that the present study should be regarded as a foundational contribution, where operator simplicity is intentional rather than limiting.

Future works

This study introduces the 2DLPFV combined with MCDM methods. The present framework assumes fixed linguistic term sets and a static reliability axis within a decision round. Future research will focus on developing systematic calibration methods to map linguistic labels to Pythagorean coordinates on both axes, and on extending the reliability dimension to a temporal setting so that confidence can be updated dynamically as new evidence becomes available without requiring full re-elicitation of expert assessments.

The current analysis also assumes independence across both criteria and experts. In practical applications, attributes may be synergistic or redundant, and expert opinions may be correlated through shared sources of information. Future work will incorporate dependence modeling within the 2 dimension Pythagorean framework by introducing interaction-aware transformations or coupling mechanisms on the two axes. This will allow complementarities and conflicts to be reflected more faithfully during aggregation while maintaining the interpretability and transparency of the existing operator family.

Conclusion

In this paper, the UAV contribution assessment problem under 2-dimension linguistic Pythagorean fuzzy environment is studied, and a novel multi-criteria decision-making method for UAV contribution assessment is proposed. Firstly, the formal definition of the 2DLPFVs is presented, which combines the 2DLVs and LPFVs, thus enabling a more accurate and reliable approach to model uncertain and fuzzy information. Then, the basic operational rules, as well as the score and accuracy functions of 2DLPFVs are defined, and some special cases are illustrated. Next, a series of 2DLPFV aggregation operators, including the 2DLPFWA operator, 2DLPFOWA operator, 2DLPFWG operator and 2DLPFOWG operator are presented and some desirable properties, such as idempotency, monotonicity and boundedness are discussed, thus allowing the aggregation of information from different sources in the form of 2DLPFVs.

Furthermore, on the basis of these, a novel multi-criteria decision-making method for UAV contribution assessment is proposed, where the assessments provided by the experts are expressed in the form of 2DLPFVs. Finally, a numerical case on UAV selection based on the contribution degree is presented to show the process and effectiveness of the proposed method, and the effect of different aggregation operators on the assessment results is discussed. Moreover, a comparative analysis between the proposed method and two other methods is conducted to show the flexibility and validity of the proposed method. It can be concluded that the proposed method is flexible and reliable in representing the uncertain and fuzzy assessments using 2DLPFVs, and it presents an effective way for MCDM problems under uncertainty.

The main novelty of this study mainly includes the following:

(1) The concept of 2DLPFV is introduced by integrating LPFV and 2DLV, which enables a more effective and flexible representation of uncertain information.

(2) The basic operations and aggregation operators for 2DLPFV are defined and proved, thus providing basic standards for computing with 2DLPFV.

(3) A novel UAV contribution assessment method based on 2DLPFV is proposed, where the 2DLPFV is used to represent the uncertain judgments of experts. The proposed method is the first one that is devoted to UAV contribution assessment under uncertainty.

However, it is also worth noting that there are some limitations to this study. First, the proposed 2DLPFV mainly considers the uncertainty of experts’ judgments, however, how to transform other forms of judgments into 2DLPFV could be further investigated. Second, the case study only focused on four assessment attributes, which may not be sufficient enough for some complex cases, and more assessment attributes could be studied.

In the future, it is meaningful to further study the distance and similarity measures of the 2DLPFVs, and studies on the further extension of the aggregation operators could also be a focus. Moreover, applications of the proposed method in different fields such as transportation problems, shortest path problems and fuzzy linear programming problems could also be explored. Moreover, the proposed method could also be extended to bio-inspired domains, such as bat-inspired acoustic localization, where the processing and integration of uncertain echo information together with confidence levels are crucial for robust and interpretable localization performance.
